-----|----------|-------|
| $A$ | area | in² |
| $b$ | width | in. |
| $C_w$ | warping constant | in⁶ |
| $d$ | depth | in. |
| $d_1$ | distance between flange fillets on a web | in. |
| $I$ | moment of inertia | in⁴ |
| $J$ | torsion constant | in⁴ |
| $r$ | radius of gyration | in. |
| $r_o$ | polar radius of gyration about the shear center | in. |
| $R$ | radius | in. |
| $R_b$ | mid-thickness radius of a pipe or tube | in. |
| $S$ | elastic section modulus | in³ |
| $t$ | thickness | in. |
| $t_f$ | flange thickness | in. |
| $t_w$ | web thickness | in. |
| $W_f$ | weight per unit length | lb/ft |
| $X$ | location of the major axis | in. |
| $x_o$ | x coordinate of shear center | in. |
| $Y$ | location of the minor axis | in. |
| $y_o$ | y coordinate of shear center | in. |
| $Z$ | plastic section modulus | in³ |

$x$ and $y$ subscripts denote the axis about which the property is taken.
The $x$ axis is the major axis. The $y$ axis is the minor axis.

# Table 2
## SECTION DESIGNATIONS

| Section | Designation | Example | Description |
|---------|-------------|---------|-------------|
| Aluminum Association<br>Standard Channels | *AC Depth* × *Wt* | AC 4 × 2.33 | C-shapes with flat flanges; designed for structural<br>efficiency |
| MC-Shapes | *MC Depth* × *Wt* | MC 3 × 2.23 | C-shapes with flat flanges |
| C-Shapes | *C Depth* × *Wt* | C 2 × 1.22 | C-shapes with flanges with a 1:6 slope on the inner<br>surface |
| Aluminum Association<br>Standard W-Shapes | *AW Depth* × *Wt* | AW 12 × 11.7 | I-shapes with flat flanges; designed for structural<br>efficiency |
| W-Shapes | *W Nominal Depth* × *Wt* | W 12 × 13.8 | I-shapes with flat flanges |
| S-Shapes | *S Depth* × *Wt* | S 10 × 12.1 | I-shapes with flanges with a 1:6 slope on the inner<br>surface |
| Angles | L *long leg* × *short leg* ×<br>*thickness* | L 3 × 2 × ¼ | L shaped product with a fillet at the junction of the<br>legs and radii on the inside tips of the legs |
| Square End Angles | LS *long leg* × *short leg* ×<br>*thickness* | LS 3 × 3 × 1/8 | L shaped product with small radii at the corners |
| Tees | T *Depth* × *Width* × *Wt* | T 2.50 × 2.50 × 1.91 | T shapes |
| Zees | Z *Depth* × *Width* × *Wt* | Z 4.00 × 3.19 × 4.32 | Z shapes |
| Plates | PL *Thickness* × *Width* | PL 0.375 × 60 | Rolled product with a rectangular cross section at<br>least 0.25 in. thick |
| Rods | RD *Diameter* | RD 0.500 | Solid product with a circular cross section at least<br>0.375 in. in diameter |
| Square Bars | SQ *Side dimension* | SQ 4 | Solid product with a square cross section at least<br>0.375 in. on a side |
| Pipes | NPS *size* × SCH *schedule no.* | NPS 4 × SCH 40 | Tube in standardized outside diameters and wall<br>thicknesses |
| Round Tubes | *Outside diameter* OD × *wall*<br>*thickness* WALL | 4 OD × 0.125 WALL | Hollow product with a circular cross section |
| Rectangular Tubes | RT *long side* × *short side* × *wall*<br>*thickness* | RT 6 × 4 × ¼ | Hollow product with a rectangular cross section<br>(including square tube) |

In the 2020 ADM, shape designations for channels and I-shapes changed. Channels with flanges sloped 1:6 are designated C-shapes, Aluminum Association standard channels are designated AC-shapes, and all other channels are designated MC-shapes. I-shapes with flanges sloped 1:6 are designated S-shapes, Aluminum Association standard I-shapes are designated AW-shapes, and all other I-shapes are designated W-shapes.

# Table 1-4
## MEMBER NOMINAL STRENGTHS IN THE *SPECIFICATION FOR ALUMINUM STRUCTURES*

| Type of Stress | Spec Section | Limit State | No Weld | Transverse Weld | Longitudinal Weld |
|---------------|--------------|-------------|---------|-----------------|-------------------|
| **Axial Tension** | D.2a | yielding $P_n =$ | $F_{ty}A_g$ | $F_{ty}A_g$ | $F_{ty}(A_g - A_{wz}) + F_{tyw}A_{wz}$ |
| | D.2b | rupture $P_n =$ | $\frac{F_{tu}A_e}{k_t}$ | $\frac{F_{tu}(A_e - A_{wez}) + F_{tuw}A_{wez}}{k_t}$ | $\frac{F_{tu}(A_e - A_{wez}) + F_{tyw}A_{wez}}{k_t}$ |
| **Axial Compression** | E.2 buckling | member $P_n =$ | $P_{no} = F_{co}A_g$ | welds at ends $P_{nwo}$ | welds not at ends $P_{nwo} = F_{co}A_g$ | $P_{nwo}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{cw}A_{wez}}{A_g}$ |
| | B.5.4 | local buckling $F_c =$ | $F_{co}$ | $F_{co}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{cw}A_{wez}}{A_g}$ if $b/t \leq \lambda_1$, $F_c = F_{co}$ | $F_{co}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{cw}A_{wez}}{A_g}$ |
| **Flexure** | F.2 | yielding $M_n =$ | $ZF_{cy} \leq 1.5S_c F_{cy}$ and $1.5SF_{cy}$ | | |
| | F.2 | rupture $M_n =$ | $\frac{ZF_u}{k_t}$ | | |
| | B.5.4 | local buckling $F_c =$ | $F_{co}$ | $F_{co}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{cw}A_{wez}}{A_g}$ | $F_{co}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{cw}A_{wez}}{A_g}$ |
| | B.5.5 | local buckling $F_n =$ | $F_{bo}$ | $F_{bo}(1 - \frac{A_{wbz}}{A_{gc}}) + \frac{F_{bw}A_{wbzc}}{A_{gc}}$ | $F_{bo}(1 - \frac{A_{wbz}}{A_{gc}}) + \frac{F_{bw}A_{wbzc}}{A_{gc}}$ |
| | F.4 | lateral-torsional buckling $M_{nwb} =$ | $M_{nmbo}$ | welds at ends $M_{nwbo}$ | welds not at ends $M_{nwbw}$ | $M_{nmbo}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_I}) + \frac{M_{nmbw}A_{wz}}{A_I}$ |
| **Shear** | G.1 | yielding and buckling $V_n =$ | $F_{so}A_v$ | $F_{so}(A_v - A_{wz}) + F_{sw}A_{wz}$ | $F_{so}(A_v - A_{wz}) + F_{sw}A_{wz}$ |
| | G.2 G.3 | rupture $V_n =$ | $\frac{F_{su}A_v}{k_t}$ | $\frac{F_{su}(A_v - A_{wz}) + F_{suw}A_{wz}}{k_t}$ | $\frac{F_{su}(A_v - A_{wz}) + F_{suw}A_{wz}}{k_t}$ |
| **Torsion** | H.2 | rupture $F_n =$ | $\frac{F_{su}}{k_t}$ | $\frac{F_{su}}{k_t}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{suw}A_{wz}}{A_g}$ | $\frac{F_{su}}{k_t}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{suw}A_{wz}}{A_g}$ |
| | H.2 | yielding buckling $F_n =$ | $F_{so}$ | $F_{so}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{sw}A_{wz}}{A_g}$ | $F_{so}(1 - \frac{A_{wz}}{A_g}) + \frac{F_{sw}A_{wz}}{A_g}$ |

# Allowable Stresses for Building-Type Structures

Tables 2-1 through 2-24 and 2-1W through 2-24W

1. These tables provide allowable stresses $F/\Omega$ for building-type structures determined in accordance with the *Specification for Aluminum Structures*.
2. For tubes with circumferential welds, these tables apply for $R_t /t < 20$.

① The radii listed are the minimum recommended for bending sheets and plates without fracture in a standard press brake with air bend dies. Other types of bending operations will require larger radii or permit smaller radii. The minimum permissible radii will also vary with the design and condition of the tooling.

② Alclad sheet in the heat-treatable alloys can be bent over slightly smaller radii than the corresponding tempers of the bare alloy.

③ Heat-treatable alloys can be formed over appreciably smaller radii immediately after solution heat treatment.

④ The H112 temper (applicable to non-heat treatable alloys) is supplied in the as-fabricated condition without special property control but usually can be formed over radii applicable to the H14 (or H34) temper or smaller.

⑤ The reference test method is ASTM E290.

⑥ Tempers T361 and T861 formerly designated T36 and T86, respectively.

# BEAM FORMULAS

## BEAMS

**Flexural stress at extreme fiber:** $f = Mc/I = M/S$
- $M$ = bending moment
- $I$ = moment of inertia
- $S = I/c$ = section modulus
- $c$ = distance to extreme fiber

**Flexural stress at any fiber:** $f = My/I$
- $y$ = distance from neutral axis to fiber.

**Average vertical shear stress:** $v = V/A$
- $V$ = shear load
- $A$ = area of cross section

**Horizontal shearing stress at any section A-A:** $v = VQ/Ib$
- $Q$ = static moment about the neutral axis of the entire section of that portion of the cross section lying outside of section A-A
- $b$ = width at section A-A

(Intensity of vertical shear is equal to that of horizontal shear acting normal to it at the same point and both are usually a maximum at mid-height of beam.)

**Slope and deflection at any point:** $EI \frac{d^2y}{dx^2} = M$ (Basic differential equation for beam)

$x$ and $y$ are abscissa and ordinate respectively of a point on the neutral axis, referred to axes of rectangular co-ordinates through a selected point of support. (First integration gives slopes; second integration gives deflections. Constants of integration must be determined.)

## CONTINUOUS BEAMS (Theorem of Three Moments)

**Uniform load:** $M_a \frac{l_1}{I_1} + 2M_b\left(\frac{l_1}{I_1} + \frac{l_2}{I_2}\right) + M_c \frac{l_2}{I_2} = -\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{w_1 l_1^3}{I_1} + \frac{w_2 l_2^3}{I_2}\right)$

**Concentrated loads:**

$$M_a \frac{l_1}{I_1} + 2M_b\left(\frac{l_1}{I_1} + \frac{l_2}{I_2}\right) + M_c \frac{l_2}{I_2} = -\frac{P_1 a_1 b_1}{l_1}\left(1 + \frac{a_1}{l_1}\right) - \frac{P_2 a_2 b_2}{l_2}\left(1 + \frac{b_2}{l_2}\right)$$

**Considering any two consecutive spans in any continuous structure:**
- $M_a, M_b, M_c$ = moments at left, center, and right supports, respectively, of any pair of adjacent spans
- $l_1$ and $l_2$ = lengths of left and right spans, respectively, of the pair.
- $I_1$ and $I_2$ = moments of inertia of left and right spans, respectively.
- $w_1$ and $w_2$ = loads per unit of length on left and right spans, respectively.
- $P_1$ and $P_2$ = concentrated loads on left and right spans, respectively.
- $a_1$ and $a_2$ = distance of concentrated loads from left support in left and right spans, respectively.
- $b_1$ and $b_2$ = distance of concentrated loads from right support in left and right spans, respectively.

The above equations are for beams with moment of inertia constant in each span but differing in different spans, continuous over three or more supports. By writing such an equation for each successive pair of spans and introducing the known values (usually zero) of end moments, all other moments can be found.

## COLUMNS

**Concentrically loaded: stress** $f = P/A$; $P$ = column load, $A$ = area of cross section

**Eccentrically loaded: stress** $f = P/A (1 + Mc/I) = P/A (1 + ec/r^2)$

**Bending in plane of principal axis.** Deflection not considered.
- $e$ = eccentricity of load, $r$ = radius of gyration.

## CASE 1.

**Concentrated load $P$ at center**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with a concentrated load P applied at center (L/2). The diagram includes the beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂ at both ends, load P at center, shear force diagrams V₁ and V₂, maximum moment Mmax at center, and maximum deflection at center)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = \frac{P}{2}$

**Maximum shear forces:**
$$V_1 = +P/2; \quad V_2 = -P/2$$

**Maximum bending moment:**
$$M_{\max} = \frac{PL}{4} \text{ at center}$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{PL^3}{48EI}$ at center

$$\text{def.} = \frac{Px}{48EI}(3L^2 - 4x^2), \quad 0 \leq x \leq \frac{L}{2}$$

## CASE 2.

**Two equal concentrated loads $P$ equi-distant from the center**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with two equal concentrated loads P applied symmetrically at distance a from each support. The diagram includes the beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, two loads P at distance a from supports, shear diagrams, maximum moment Mmax between loads, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = P$

**Maximum shear forces:**
$$V_1 = +P; \quad V_2 = -P$$

**Maximum bending moment:**
$$M_{\max} = Pa, \text{ between loads}$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{Pa}{24EI}(3L^2 - 4a^2)$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{Px}{6EI}(3La - 3a^2 - x^2), \quad 0 \leq x \leq a$$

$$= \frac{Pa}{6EI}(3Lx - 3x^2 - a^2), \quad a \leq x \leq (L-a)$$

## CASE 3.

**Concentrated load $P$ at any point**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with a concentrated load P applied at distance a from left support. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, load P at distance a from left support, distance b to right support, shear diagrams, maximum moment Mmax at load location, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{Pb}{L}$; $R_2 = \frac{Pa}{L}$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

**Maximum bending moment:**
$$M_{\max} = \frac{Pab}{L}, \text{ at } x = a$$

$$M = \frac{Pbx}{L}, \quad 0 \leq x \leq a; \quad M = \frac{Pa}{L}(L-x), \quad a \leq x \leq L$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{Pab(L+b)\sqrt{3a(L+b)}}{27EIL}$

at $x = \sqrt{a(L+b)/3}$ when $a > b$

**deflection under load** = $\frac{Pa^2 b^2}{3EIL}$, $x = a$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{Pbx}{6EIL}(L^2 - b^2 - x^2), \quad 0 \leq x \leq a$$

## CASE 4.

**Two unequal concentrated loads unsymmetrically located**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with two unequal concentrated loads P₁ and P₂ applied at distances a and c from left support, with distance b between the loads. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, loads P₁ at distance a and P₂ at distance a+b+c from left support, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, and moment diagrams M₁ and M₂)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{P_1(L-a) + P_2 c}{L}$

$$R_2 = \frac{P_2(L-c) + P_1 a}{L}$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$
$$V_2 = -R_2$$

**Bending moments:**
$$M_1 = R_1 a, \text{ maximum if } R_1 < P_1$$
$$M_2 = R_2 c, \text{ maximum if } R_2 < P_2$$

## CASE 5.

**Two equal concentrated loads unsymmetrically located**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with two equal concentrated loads P applied at distances a and c from supports, with distance b between the loads. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, two equal loads P at asymmetric locations, shear diagrams, and moment diagrams)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{P(L-a+c)}{L}$

$$R_2 = \frac{P(L-c+a)}{L}$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$
$$V_2 = -R_2$$

**Bending moments:**
$$M_1 = R_1 a, \text{ maximum if } a > c$$
$$M_2 = R_2 c, \text{ maximum if } a < c$$
$$M = R_1 x - P(x-a), \quad a \leq x \leq (a+b)$$

## CASE 6.

**Uniformly distributed loading of $w$ lb/in total load $W = wL$**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with uniformly distributed load w lb/in. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing triangular distribution, bending moment diagram showing parabolic curve with Mmax at center, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, uniform load w, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, maximum moment at center, and maximum deflection at center)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = \frac{wL}{2} = \frac{W}{2}$

**Shear** = $w\left(\frac{L}{2} - x\right)$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum bending moment** = $\frac{wL^2}{8} = \frac{WL}{8}$, at center

$$M = \frac{wx}{2}(L-x) = \frac{Wx}{2L}(L-x), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{5wL^4}{384EI} = \frac{5WL^3}{384EI}$, at center

$$\text{def.} = \frac{wx}{24EI}(L^3 - 2Lx^2 + x^3), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 7.

**Distributed load increasing uniformly to one end**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with triangular distributed load increasing from left to right, maximum loading w lb/in at right end. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram with Mmax at 0.5774L, and deflection curve with maximum deflection at 0.5193L.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, triangular load distribution, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Total load** $W = \frac{wL}{2}$, max. loading = $w$ lb/in

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{W}{3} = \frac{wL}{6}$

$$R_2 = \frac{2W}{3} = \frac{wL}{3}$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

$$V = \frac{W}{3} - \frac{Wx^2}{L^2}, \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

**Maximum bending moment** = $0.1283WL$, $x = 0.5774L$

$$M = \frac{Wx}{3L^2}(L^2 - x^2), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

**Maximum deflection** = $0.01304 \frac{WL^3}{EI}$, $x = 0.5193L$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{Wx}{180EIL^2}(3x^4 - 10L^2x^2 + 7L^4), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 8.

**Distributed load increasing toward center**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with distributed load increasing toward center in triangular pattern from both ends. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram with Mmax at center, and deflection curve with maximum deflection at center.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, symmetric triangular load distribution peaking at center, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Total load** $W = \frac{wL}{2}$, max. loading = $w$ lb/in

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = \frac{W}{2} = \frac{wL}{4}$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

$$V = \frac{W}{2L^2}(L^2 - 4x^2), \quad 0 \leq x \leq \frac{L}{2}$$

**Maximum bending moment** = $\frac{WL}{6} = \frac{wL^2}{12}$

$$M = \frac{Wx}{2}\left(1 - \frac{4x^2}{3L^2}\right), \quad 0 \leq x \leq \frac{L}{2}$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{WL^3}{60EI} = \frac{wL^4}{120EI}$, $x = \frac{L}{2}$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{Wx}{480EIL^2}(5L^2 - 4x^2)^2, \quad 0 \leq x \leq \frac{L}{2}$$

## CASE 9.

**Load uniformly distributed at left end of beam**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with uniform distributed load w over length a at left end. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram with Mmax at R₁/w, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, uniform load over distance a from left support, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Total load** = $wa$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{wa}{2L}(2L-a)$; $R_2 = \frac{wa^2}{2L}$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$
$$V = wx, \quad 0 \leq x \leq a$$

**Maximum bending moment:**

$$= \frac{wa^2}{8L^2}(2L-a)^2, \quad x = \frac{R_1}{w}$$

$$M = R_1 x - \frac{wx^2}{2}, \quad 0 \leq x \leq a$$

**Def.** = $\frac{wx}{24EIL}[a^3(2L-a)^2 - 2ax^2(2L-a) + Lx^3]$,

$$0 \leq x \leq a$$

$$= \frac{wa^2(L-x)}{24EIL}(4Lx - 2x^2 - a^2), \quad a \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 10.

**Partially distributed uniform load**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with uniform distributed load w over length b between distances a and c from left support. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram with Mmax at a + R₁/w, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, uniform load w over distance b in middle portion, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Total load** = $wb$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{wb}{2L}(2c+b)$

$$R_2 = \frac{wb}{2L}(2a+b)$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$
$$= R_1 - w(x-a), \quad a \leq x \leq a+b$$

**Maximum bending moment**

$$= \frac{wb}{8L^2}(2c+b)[4aL + b(2c+b)], \quad x = a + \frac{R_1}{w}$$

**Deflection:**

$$= \frac{w}{24EI}\left[\frac{b(b+2c)x}{L}\right]\left[-(2x^2 + 2a(2L-a) + b(b+2c))\right]$$

for $0 \leq x \leq a$

$$= \frac{w}{24EI}\left\{(x-a)^4 + \left[\frac{b(b+2c)x}{L}\right] - 2x^2 + 2a(2L-a) + b(b+2c)\right\}$$

for $a \leq x \leq a+b$

## CASE 11.

**Partially distributed uniform load at each end**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with uniform distributed loads w₁ over length a at left end and w₂ over length c at right end, with unloaded length b in middle. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, loads w₁ and w₂ at ends, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram)

**Total load** = $w_1 a + w_2 c$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{w_1 a(2L-a) + w_2 c^2}{2L}$

$$R_2 = \frac{w_2 c(2L-c) + w_1 a^2}{2L}$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

**Maximum bending moment**

$$= \frac{R_1^2}{2w_1}, \text{ when } R_1 < w_1 a$$

$$M = R_1 x - \frac{w_1 x^2}{2}, \quad 0 \leq x \leq a$$

$$M = R_1 x - \frac{w_1 a}{2}(2x-a), \quad a \leq x \leq a+b$$

$$M = R_2(L-x) - \frac{w_2(L-x)^2}{2}, \quad a+b \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 12.

**Two equal and opposite moments, $M_o$, at ends**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with equal and opposite moments Mo applied at both ends. The diagram includes beam elevation, constant bending moment diagram with Mmax = Mo, and deflection curve with maximum at center.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, end moments Mo, constant moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Reactions: both zero**

**Shear forces: zero at all points**

**Maximum bending moment** = $M_o$
$$M = M_o \text{ at all points}$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{M_o L^2}{8EI}$, $x = \frac{L}{2}$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{M_o x}{2EI}(L-x), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 12A.

**Trapezoidally distributed load:**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with trapezoidal distributed load, with equal overhang distances a on both ends beyond the load. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, bending moment diagram with Mmax at center, and deflection curve.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, trapezoidal load distribution with max load w at center, shear diagrams, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Total Load:** $W = w(L-a)$

**Max. Load:** $w$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = W/2$, $R_2 = W/2$

**Shear Forces:** $V_1 = R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

**Maximum bending moment** =

$$\frac{W}{24}(3L^2 - 4a^2), \quad x = \frac{L}{2}$$

**Maximum deflection** =

$$\frac{wL^4}{1920EI}\left[25 - 40\left(\frac{a}{L}\right)^2 + 16\left(\frac{a}{L}\right)^4\right], \quad x = \frac{L}{2}$$

## CASE 13.

**Moment, $M_o$, applied at $x = a$**

![Diagram showing a simply supported beam of length L with moment Mo applied at distance a from left support, with distance b to right support. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagrams Ma and Mb, and deflection curve with deflections d₁ and d₂.](Beam with supports R₁ and R₂, applied moment Mo at distance a, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagrams, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = +\frac{M_o}{L}$; $R_2 = -\frac{M_o}{L}$

**Shear force** $V = +\frac{M_o}{L}$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Bending moment:** $M_a = \frac{M_o a}{L}$, $M_b = -\frac{M_o b}{L}$

$$M_a - M_b = M_o$$

**Deflection:**

$$d_1 = \frac{M_o}{6EIL}\left[\left(6a - \frac{3a^2}{L} - 2L\right)x - \frac{x^3}{L}\right], \quad 0 \leq x \leq a$$

$$d_2 = \frac{M_o}{6EIL}\left[3a^2 + 3x^2 - \frac{x^3}{L} - \left(2L + \frac{3a^2}{L}\right)x\right], \quad a \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 14.

**Concentrated load, $P$, at free end of cantilever beam**

![Diagram showing a cantilever beam fixed at left end with length L and concentrated load P at free end. The diagram includes beam elevation, constant shear force diagram V, linearly increasing bending moment diagram with Mmax at fixed end, and deflection curve with maximum deflection at free end.](Cantilever beam with fixed support R at left, load P at free end, shear diagram, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Reaction:** $R = P$, at fixed end

**Shear force** $V = -P$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum bending moment** = $-PL$, $x = L$

**Bending moment:** $M = -Px$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{PL^3}{3EI}$, at free end, $x = o$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{P}{6EI}(2L^3 - 3L^2x + x^3), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 15.

**Moment, $-M_o$, at free end of cantilever beam**

![Diagram showing a cantilever beam fixed at left end with length L and moment Mo applied at free end. The diagram includes beam elevation, zero shear force, constant bending moment diagram with Mmax = -Mo, and deflection curve with maximum deflection at free end.](Cantilever beam with fixed support R at left, moment Mo at free end, constant moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Reaction:** $R = o$

**Shear force:** $V = o$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum bending moment** = $-M_o$

**Bending moment** = $-M_o$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{M_o L^2}{2EI}$, at free end

$$\text{def.} = \frac{M_o(L-x)^2}{2EI}, \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 16.

**Uniformly distributed loading of $w$ lb/in**

![Diagram showing a cantilever beam fixed at left end with length L and uniformly distributed load w. The diagram includes beam elevation, linearly varying shear force diagram, parabolic bending moment diagram with Mmax at fixed end, and deflection curve with maximum deflection at free end.](Cantilever beam with fixed support R at left, uniform load w, shear diagram V, moment diagram with Mmax at fixed end, and deflection curve)

**Total load** $W = wL$

**Reaction:** $R = W = wL$

**Maximum shear force** = $-W = -wL$, at $x = L$

**Shear force** = $-wx$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum bending moment** = $-\frac{WL}{2} = -\frac{wL^2}{2}$, at fixed end

**Bending moment:** $M = -\frac{Wx^2}{2L} = -\frac{wx^2}{2}$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{WL^3}{8EI} = \frac{wL^4}{8EI}$, at free end

$$\text{def} = \frac{w}{24EI}(x^4 - 4L^3x + 3L^4), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 17.

**Distributed loading, increasing uniformly to fixed end**

![Diagram showing a cantilever beam fixed at left end with length L and triangular distributed load increasing from zero at free end to maximum w at fixed end. The diagram includes beam elevation, parabolic shear force diagram, cubic bending moment diagram with Mmax at fixed end, and deflection curve with maximum deflection at free end.](Cantilever beam with fixed support R at left, triangular load distribution, shear diagram, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Total load** = $W = \frac{wL}{2}$

**Reaction:** $R = W = \frac{wL}{2}$

**Maximum shear force** = $-W = -\frac{wL}{2}$, at $x = L$

**Shear force** = $-\frac{Wx^2}{L^2}$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum bending moment** = $-\frac{WL}{3}$, at fixed end

**Bending moment** = $-\frac{Wx^3}{3L^2}$, $0 \leq x \leq L$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{WL^3}{15EI}$, at free end

$$\text{def.} = \frac{W}{60EIL^2}(x^5 - 5Lx^4 + 4L^5), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 18.

**Concentrated load, $P$, inboard of free end**

![Diagram showing a cantilever beam fixed at left end with length L and concentrated load P at distance (L-a) from fixed end, or distance a from free end. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram with Mmax at fixed end, and deflection curve with maximum deflection at free end.](Cantilever beam with fixed support R at left, load P at distance a from free end, shear diagram, moment diagram, and deflection curve)

**Reaction:** $R = P$

**Shear force:** $V = 0$, $0 \leq x \leq a$
$$V = -P, \quad a < x \leq L$$

**Maximum bending moment** = $-P(L-a)$, at fixed end

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{P(L-a)^2}{6EI}(2L+a)$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{P}{6EI}(2L^3 - 3L^2x + x^3), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 19.

**Uniformly distributed loading of $w$ lb/in**

![Diagram showing a beam with overhangs on both ends, with uniform distributed load w across entire length. Supports R₁ and R₂ are at distance a from each end. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagrams showing negative moments at supports and positive moment at center, and deflection curve.](Beam with equal overhangs a on both sides, supports at interior positions, uniform load w, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagrams at supports and center, and deflection curve)

**Total load,** $W = wL$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = \frac{W}{2}$

**Shear forces:**
$$V = wa, \text{ just outboard of supports}$$
$$V = \frac{w}{2}(L - 2a), \text{ just inboard of supports}$$

**Bending moments:**

$$M_B = -\frac{wa^2}{2} = -\frac{Wa^2}{2L}, \text{ at supports}$$

$$M_c = \frac{W}{8}(L - 4a), \text{ at center}$$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{W(L-2a)^3}{384EI}\left[\frac{5}{L}(L-2a) - \frac{24}{L}\left(\frac{a^2}{L-2a}\right)\right], \text{ at center}$$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{W(L-2a)^4}{24EIL}\left[-1 + 6\left(\frac{a}{L-2a}\right)^2 + 3\left(\frac{a}{L-2a}\right)^4\right],$$
at ends

## CASE 20.

**Concentrated load, $P$, at end of overhang**

![Diagram showing a beam with overhang on right end, with concentrated load P at end of overhang. Supports R₁ and R₂ are at distance L apart, with overhang distance a. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram, and deflection curve showing maximum downward and upward deflections.](Beam with overhang a on right side, load P at end of overhang, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram with max at right support, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = -\frac{Pa}{L}$

$$R_2 = \frac{P}{L}(L+a)$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = -\frac{Pa}{L}$
$$V_2 = P$$

**Maximum bending moment:**
$$M_{\max} = -Pa, \text{ at right support}$$

**Deflections:**

**Maximum downward def.** = $\frac{Pa^2}{3EI}(L+a)$, at load

**Maximum upward deflection; at** $x = \frac{L}{\sqrt{3}}$

$$= \frac{PaL^2}{9\sqrt{3}EI} = 0.06415 \frac{PaL^2}{EI}$$

## CASE 21.

**Concentrated load, $P$, between supports**

![Diagram showing a beam with overhang on right end, with concentrated load P at distance b from left support R₁. Supports R₁ and R₂ are at distance L apart, with overhang distance a on right. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram with Mmax at load location, and deflection curve.](Beam with overhang a, load P at distance b from left support, shear diagrams, moment diagram with max at load, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{Pb}{L}$; $R_2 = \frac{P(L-b)}{L}$

**Maximum bending moment** = $\frac{Pb(L-b)}{L}$, at load

**Maximum downward deflection**

$$= \frac{Pb(L^2 - b^2)\sqrt{3}(L^2 - b^2)}{27EIL}, \text{ at } x = \frac{\sqrt{3}(L^2 - b^2)}{3}$$

**Maximum upward deflection**

$$= -\frac{Pab}{6EI}\left(2L + \frac{b^2}{L} - 3b\right), \text{ at end of overhang}$$

## CASE 22.

**Two equal loads, $P$, at ends of overhang**

![Diagram showing a beam with overhangs on both ends, with concentrated loads P at each end of the overhangs. Supports R₁ and R₂ are at distance L apart, with equal overhangs a on both sides. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram showing negative moment between supports, and deflection curve.](Beam with equal overhangs a, loads P at both ends, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram with max between supports, and deflection curve showing downward deflections d₁ at loads and upward deflection d₂ at center)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = P$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = -P$; $V_2 = +P$

**Maximum bending moment** = $-Pa$, between supports

**Deflections:**

$$d_1 = \frac{Pa^2}{3EI}\left(a + \frac{3}{2}L\right), \text{ at point of load}$$

$$d_2 = -\frac{PL^2a}{8EI}, \text{ at center point}$$

## CASE 23.

**Uniformly distributed loading, $w$ lb/in on overhang**

![Diagram showing a beam with overhang on right end, with uniform distributed load w on the overhang only. Supports R₁ and R₂ are at distance L apart, with overhang distance a. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram, and deflection curve.](Beam with overhang a, uniform load w on overhang, shear diagrams V₁ and V₂, moment diagram with max at right support, and deflection curve)

**Total load** = $wa$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = -\frac{wa^2}{2L}$

$$R_2 = \frac{wa}{2L}(2L+a)$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = R_1$
$$V_2 = wa$$

**Maximum bending moment:**

$$M_{\max} = -\frac{wa^2}{2}, \text{ at right support}$$

**Deflection at end of overhang:**

$$d_1 = \frac{wa^3}{24EI}(4L+3a)$$

**Maximum deflection between supports:**

$$d_2 = -\frac{wa^2L^3}{18\sqrt{3}EI} = -0.03208\frac{wa^2L^3}{EI}$$

## CASE 24.

**Uniformly distributed loading, $w$ lb/in between supports**

![Diagram showing a beam with overhang on right end, with uniform distributed load w between supports only. Supports R₁ and R₂ are at distance L apart, with overhang distance a (unloaded). The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram, bending moment diagram with Mmax at center between supports, and deflection curve.](Beam with overhang a, uniform load w between supports only, shear diagrams, moment diagram with max at center, and deflection curve showing downward deflection d₂ between supports and upward deflection d₁ at end of overhang)

**Total load** = $wL$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = \frac{wL}{2}$

**Maximum bending moment** = $\frac{wL^2}{8}$, $x = \frac{L}{2}$

**Deflection at end of overhang:**

$$d_1 = -\frac{wL^3a}{24EI}$$

**Maximum deflection between supports:**

$$d_2 = \frac{5wL^4}{384EI}$$

## CASE 25.

**Uniformly distributed load, $w$ lb/in**

![Diagram showing a fixed-fixed beam (both ends fixed) with length L and uniformly distributed load w. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, bending moment diagram showing negative moments at ends and positive moment Mc at center, and deflection curve.](Fixed-fixed beam with uniform load w, shear diagrams, moment diagrams showing negative moments at ends and positive moment at center, and deflection curve)

**Total load** $W = wL$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = \frac{W}{2}$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +\frac{W}{2}$

$$V_2 = -\frac{W}{2}$$

**Maximum (negative) bending moment**

$$M_{\max} = -\frac{wL^2}{12} = -\frac{WL}{12}, \text{ at end}$$

**Maximum (positive) bending moment**

$$M_c = \frac{wL^2}{24} = \frac{WL}{24}, \text{ at center}$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{wL^4}{384EI} = \frac{WL^3}{384EI}$, at center

$$\text{def.} = \frac{wx^2}{24EI}(L-x)^2, \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 26.

**Concentrated load, $P$, at center**

![Diagram showing a fixed-fixed beam with length L and concentrated load P at center (L/2). The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, bending moment diagram showing positive moment Mmax at center and negative moments at ends, and deflection curve.](Fixed-fixed beam with load P at center, shear diagrams, moment diagrams with positive moment at center and negative moments at ends, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = R_2 = \frac{P}{2}$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +\frac{P}{2}$; $V_2 = -\frac{P}{2}$

**Maximum bending moment**

$$M_{\max} = \frac{PL}{8}, \text{ at center}$$

$$M_{\max} = -\frac{PL}{8}, \text{ at ends}$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{PL^3}{192EI}$, at center

$$\text{def.} = \frac{Px^2}{48EI}(3L - 4x), \quad 0 \leq x \leq \frac{L}{2}$$

## CASE 27.

**Concentrated load, $P$, at any point**

![Diagram showing a fixed-fixed beam with length L and concentrated load P at distance a from left support, with distance b to right support. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, bending moment diagram showing positive moment at load location and negative moments M₁ and M₂ at ends, and deflection curve.](Fixed-fixed beam with load P at any point, shear diagrams, moment diagrams with maximum at load and negative moments at ends, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{Pb^2}{L^3}(3a+b)$

$$R_2 = \frac{Pa^2}{L^3}(3b+a)$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

**Bending moments:**

$$M_1 = -\frac{Pab^2}{L^2}, \text{ max. when } a < b$$

$$M_2 = -\frac{Pa^2b}{L^2}, \text{ max. when } a > b$$

$$M_p = +\frac{2Pa^2b^2}{L^3}, \text{ at point of load}$$

**Deflection** = $\frac{Pa^3b^3}{3EIL^3}$, at point of load

**Max. def.** = $\frac{2Pa^3b^3}{3EI(3a+b)^3}$, at $x = \frac{2aL}{3a+b}$, for $a > b$

## CASE 28.

**Uniformly distributed load, $w$ lb/in**

![Diagram showing a fixed-pinned beam (fixed at left, pinned at right) with length L and uniformly distributed load w. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, bending moment diagram showing negative moment at left end and positive moment at 3/8 L, and deflection curve.](Fixed-pinned beam with uniform load w, shear diagrams, moment diagrams with negative moment at fixed end and positive moment at 3/8 L, and deflection curve with max at 0.4215L)

**Total load** $W = wL$

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{3wL}{8}$, $R_2 = \frac{5wL}{8}$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = +R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

**Bending moments:**

**Max. negative moment** = $-\frac{wL^2}{8}$, at left end

**Max. positive moment** = $\frac{9}{128}wL^2$, $x = \frac{3}{8}L$

$$M = \frac{3wLx}{8} - \frac{wx^2}{2}, \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

**Maximum deflection** = $\frac{wL^4}{185EI}$, $x = 0.4215L$

$$\text{def.} = \frac{wx}{48EI}(L^3 - 3Lx^2 + 2x^3), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 29.

**Concentrated load, $P$, at center**

![Diagram showing a fixed-pinned beam with length L and concentrated load P at center (L/2). The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, bending moment diagram showing negative moment at fixed end and positive moment at center, and deflection curve.](Fixed-pinned beam with load P at center, shear diagrams, moment diagrams with max negative at fixed end and max positive at center, and deflection curve with max at 0.447L)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{5}{16}P$; $R_2 = \frac{11}{16}P$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

**Bending moments:**

**Max. negative moment** = $-\frac{3PL}{16}$, at fixed end

**Max. positive moment** = $\frac{5PL}{32}$, at center

**Maximum deflection** = $0.00937 \frac{PL^3}{EI}$, at $x = 0.447L$

**Deflection at center under load** = $\frac{7PL^3}{768EI}$

## CASE 30.

**Concentrated load, $P$, at any point**

![Diagram showing a fixed-pinned beam with length L and concentrated load P at distance b from fixed end (left), or distance a from pinned end (right). The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, bending moment diagram showing negative moment M₂ at fixed end and positive moment M₁ at load location, and deflection curve.](Fixed-pinned beam with load P at distance b from fixed end, shear diagrams, moment diagrams, and deflection curve with maximum downward deflection dp)

**Concentrated load, $P$, at any point**

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{Pb^2}{2L^3}(a+2L)$, $R_2 = \frac{Pa}{2L^3}(3L^2 - a^2)$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = R_1$; $V_2 = -R_2$

**Bending moments:**

**Max. negative moment,** $M_2 = -\frac{Pab}{2L^2}(a+L)$, at fixed end

**Max. positive moment,** $M_1 = \frac{Pab^2}{2L^3}(a+2L)$, at load

**Deflections:** $d_p = \frac{Pa^2b^3}{12EIL^3}(3L+a)$, at load

$$d_{\max} = \frac{Pa(L^2 - a^2)^3}{3EI(3L^2 - a^2)^2}, \text{ at } x = \frac{L^2 + a^2}{3L^2 - a^2}L, \text{ when } a < 0.414L$$

$$d_{\max} = \frac{Pab^3}{6EI}\sqrt{\frac{a}{2L+a}}, \text{ at } x = L\sqrt{\frac{a}{2L+a}}, \text{ when } a > 0.414L$$

## CASE 31.

**Concentrated load, $P$, at end of overhang**

![Diagram showing a fixed-pinned beam with overhang on right end. Fixed support on left, pinned support R₁ at distance L, with overhang distance a. Concentrated load P at end of overhang. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₂ and V₁, bending moment diagram showing negative moments M₂ and M₁, and deflection curve.](Fixed-pinned beam with overhang a, load P at end of overhang, shear diagrams, moment diagrams showing negative moments at fixed end M₂ and at pinned support M₁, and deflection curve)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = P\left(1 + \frac{3a}{2L}\right)$

$$R_2 = -\frac{3Pa}{2L}$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = -P$

$$V_2 = \frac{3Pa}{2L}$$

**Bending moments:**

$$M_1 = -Pa, \text{ at } R_1$$

$$M_2 = \frac{Pa}{2}, \text{ at fixed end}$$

**Deflection at end of overhang**

$$\text{def.} = \frac{PL^2}{EI}\left(\frac{a^2}{4L^2} + \frac{a^3}{3L^3}\right)$$

## CASE 32.

**Distributed load, $w$ lb/in. on overhang**

![Diagram showing a fixed-pinned beam with overhang on right end. Fixed support on left, pinned support R₁ at distance L, with overhang distance a. Uniformly distributed load w on overhang. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₂ and V₁, and bending moment diagram showing negative moments M₂ and M₁.](Fixed-pinned beam with overhang a, uniform load w on overhang only, shear diagrams, and moment diagrams showing negative moments)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = wa\left(1 + \frac{3a}{4L}\right)$

$$R_2 = -\frac{3wa^2}{4L}$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = -wa$

$$V_2 = \frac{3wa^2}{4L}$$

**Bending moments:**

$$M_1 = -\frac{wa^2}{2}, \text{ at } R_1$$

$$M_2 = \frac{wa^2}{4}, \text{ at fixed end}$$

## CASE 33.

**Distributed triangular loading of $w$ lb/in. maximum intensity on overhang**

![Diagram showing a fixed-pinned beam with overhang on right end. Fixed support on left, pinned support R₁ at distance L, with overhang distance a. Triangular distributed load with maximum intensity w at end of overhang, decreasing to zero at pinned support. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₂ and V₁, and bending moment diagram showing negative moments M₂ and M₁.](Fixed-pinned beam with overhang a, triangular load distribution with max w at end, shear diagrams, and moment diagrams)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{wa}{2}\left(1 + \frac{a}{2L}\right)$

$$R_2 = -\frac{wa^2}{4L}$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = -\frac{wa}{2}$

$$V_2 = \frac{wa^2}{4L}$$

**Bending moments:**

$$M_1 = -\frac{wa^2}{6}, \text{ at } R_1$$

$$M_2 = \frac{wa^2}{12}, \text{ at fixed end}$$

$$R_2 = 1.25wL$$

**Shear forces:**

$$V_1 = -V_3 = \frac{3}{8}wL$$

$$V_2 = \pm \frac{5}{8}wL$$

**Bending moments:**

$$M_{\max} = -\frac{1}{8}wL^2$$

$$M_1 = \frac{9}{128}wL^2$$

**Maximum deflection** = $0.00541\frac{wL^4}{EI}$

at $x = 0.4215L$

$$\text{Def.} = \frac{w}{48EI}(L^4x - 3Lx^3 + 2x^4), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

$$M_R = -\frac{1}{16}wL^2, \text{ at } R_2$$

$$M = \frac{wx}{16}(7L - 8x), \quad 0 \leq x \leq L$$

## CASE 39. Continuous beam of two equal spans—concentrated load, $P$, at any point on one span.

![Diagram showing a continuous beam with two equal spans L, with concentrated load P at distance a from left support in left span (distance b to center support). Three supports R₁, R₂, R₃. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagrams V₁-V₃, and bending moment diagram.](Continuous beam with two equal spans, load P at any point on one span, three supports, shear diagrams, and moment diagram)

**Reactions:** $R_1 = \frac{Pb}{4L^3}[4L^3 - a(L+a)]$

$$R_2 = \frac{Pa}{2L^3}[2L^2 + b(L+a)]$$

$$R_3 = -\frac{Pab}{4L^3}(L+a)$$

**Shear forces:** $V_1 = \frac{Pb}{4L^3}[4L^3 - a(L+a)]$

$$V_2 = -\frac{Pa}{4L^3}[4L^2 + b(L+a)]$$

$$V_3 = \frac{Pab}{4L^3}(L+a)$$

**Bending moments:** $M_{\max} = \frac{Pab}{4L^3}[4L^3 - a(L+a)]$

$$M_R = -\frac{Pab}{4L^3}(L+a)$$

## CASE 44. SIMPLE BEAM
### *One Concentrated Moving Load*

![Diagram showing a simple beam with supports RL and RR, length L, with a concentrated moving load P at distance x from left support.](Simple beam with moving load P at position x)

$$R_L \text{ max} = V_L \text{ max (at } x = 0) = P$$

$$M \text{ max}\left(\text{at point of load, when } x = \frac{L}{2}\right) = \frac{PL}{4}$$

## CASE 45. SIMPLE BEAM
### *Two Equal Concentrated Moving Loads*

![Diagram showing a simple beam with supports RL and RR, length L, with two equal concentrated loads P separated by distance a, with first load at position x from left support.](Simple beam with two equal moving loads P separated by distance a)

$$R_L \text{ max} = V_L \text{ max (at } x = 0) = P\left(2 - \frac{a}{L}\right)$$

$$M \text{ max}\begin{cases}
\text{when } a < (2-\sqrt{2})L = 0.586L \\
\text{under load 1 at } x = \frac{1}{2}\left(L - \frac{a}{2}\right)
\end{cases} = \frac{P}{2L}\left(L - \frac{a}{2}\right)^2$$

$$\text{when } a > (2-\sqrt{2})L = 0.586L \\
\text{with one load at center of span} \\
\text{(other load is then off span)} = \frac{PL}{4}$$

## CASE 46. SIMPLE BEAM
### *Two Unequal Concentrated Moving Loads*

![Diagram showing a simple beam with supports RL and RR, length L, with two unequal concentrated loads PL and PR separated by distance a, with PL at position x from left support.](Simple beam with two unequal moving loads PL and PR separated by distance a)

$$R_L \text{ max} = V_L \text{ max (at } x = 0) = P_L + P_R \frac{L-a}{L}$$

$$M \text{ max}\begin{cases}
\text{under } P_L, \text{ at } x = \frac{1}{2}\left(L - \frac{P_L a}{P_L + P_R}\right)
\end{cases} - (P_L + P_R)\frac{x^2}{L}$$

$$\left[\text{M max may occur with larger load at} \\
\text{center of span and other load off span}\right] = \frac{P_LL}{4}$$

## CASE 47.
### GENERAL RULES FOR SIMPLE BEAMS CARRYING CONCENTRATED MOVING LOADS

![Diagram showing a simple beam with supports RL and RR, length L, with multiple concentrated moving loads PL, c.g., and PR at various positions, with moment diagram shown below.](Simple beam with multiple moving loads and moment diagram)

**Maximum shear due to moving concentrated loads occurs at support when one of the loads is at that support. With several moving loads, the point of maximum shear must be determined by trial. Maximum bending moment produced by moving concentrated loads occurs under one of the loads when that load is as far from one support as the center of gravity of all the moving loads on the beam is from the other support.**

In the diagram, maximum bending moment occurs under load $P_L$ when $x = b$. It should also be noted that this condition occurs when the center line of the span is midway between the center of gravity of the loads and the nearest concentrated load.

## CASE 48. Uniformly distributed load and variable end moments.

![Diagram showing a beam with length L, uniformly distributed load wL, and variable end moments M₁ and M₂. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, and bending moment diagram showing moments M₁, M₂, Ms, and Mb.](Beam with uniform load and end moments, with shear and moment diagrams)

$$R_1 = V_1 = \frac{wL}{2} + \frac{M_1 - M_2}{L}$$

$$R_2 = V_2 = \frac{wL}{2} - \frac{M_1 - M_2}{L}$$

$$V_x = w\left(\frac{L}{2} - x\right) + \frac{M_1 - M_2}{L}$$

$$M_s \quad \left(at\, x = \frac{L}{2} + \frac{M_1 - M_2}{wL}\right) = \frac{wL^2}{8} - \frac{M_1 + M_2}{2} + \frac{(M_1 - M_2)^2}{2wL^2}$$

$$M_x = \frac{wx}{2}(L - x) + \left(\frac{M_1 - M_2}{L}\right)x - M_1$$

$$b\text{ (to locate inflection points)} = \sqrt{\frac{L^2}{4} - \left(\frac{M_1 + M_2}{w}\right) + \left(\frac{M_1 - M_2}{wL}\right)^2}$$

$$\Delta_x = \frac{wx}{24EI}\left[x^3 - \left(2L + \frac{4M_1}{wL} - \frac{4M_2}{wL}\right)x^2 + \frac{12M_1}{w}x + L^3 - \frac{8M_1L}{w} - \frac{4M_2L}{w}\right]$$

## CASE 49. Concentrated load at center and variable end moments.

![Diagram showing a beam with length L, concentrated load P at center (L/2), and variable end moments M₁ and M₂. The diagram includes beam elevation, shear force diagram showing V₁ and V₂, and bending moment diagram showing moments M₁, M₂, Ms, and Mb.](Beam with concentrated load at center and end moments, with shear and moment diagrams)

$$R_1 = V_1 = \frac{P}{2} + \frac{M_1 - M_2}{L}$$

$$R_2 = V_2 = \frac{P}{2} - \frac{M_1 - M_2}{L}$$

$$M_s\text{ (at center)} = \frac{PL}{4} - \frac{M_1 + M_2}{2}$$

$$M_x\text{ (when } x < \frac{L}{2}) = \left(\frac{P}{2} + \frac{M_1 - M_2}{L}\right)x - M_1$$

$$M_x\text{ (when } x > \frac{L}{2}) = \frac{P}{2}(L - x) + \frac{(M_1 - M_2)x}{L} - M_1$$

$$\Delta_x\text{ (when } x < \frac{L}{2}) = \frac{Px}{48EI}\left[3L^2 - 4x^2 - \frac{8(L - x)}{PL}\left[M_1(2L - x) + M_2(L + x)\right]\right]$$
